L1089: **Reviewer's Assessment:** Per FDA's bioanalytical method validation guidance (May 2018), the methods met the requirements for all tested validation parameters. The PK bioanalytical method validation and sample analyses reports are acceptable. Overall, zilucoplan and metabolites concentrations measured in plasma, urine, and feces samples from Phase 1, 2, and 3 studies are reliable.

L1095: ## 14.3.2.1. Total C5 Bioanalytical Method
L1096: The total C5 method is based on a commercially available human C5 ELISA kit which is compatible with human plasma. The C5 present in the sample is captured by anti-C5 polyclonal capture antibody precoated on 8-well strips. The bound C5 is detected by biotinylated anti-C5 polyclonal antibody and peroxidase-labeled streptavidin and developed with peroxidase substrate. The optical density at 450 nm) measured is proportional to the quantity of C5 in the sample. The total C5 concentration in test samples is derived from the standard curve (7 standard calibrators), and the results are reported as \mu\text{g/mL} of C5. The method's specificity for endogenous C5 level in human plasma is validated to a range of 6\mu\text{g/mL} to 1440\mu\text{g/mL} (Method 618811). For the context of use, the total C5 ELISA method was validated for QC performance (precision), sensitivity, linearity, hemolysis effect, bench-top/process stability, freeze-thaw stability, long-term storage stability, and parallelism. The C5 in plasma was stable for 90 days at -70^\circ\text{C}. The method performance during the study sample analysis was also evaluated by QC performance (precision).
L1097: **Reviewer's Assessment:** For the context of use, the tested validation parameters for total C5 assay method is appropriate to establish its use for sample analysis. The QC precision across 3 levels during validation was &lt;20%. The precision observed during method performance across 3 levels of QC ranged from 11.87% to 23.64%. It was noted that the method validation did not include accuracy and precision for calibration curve performance. In response (received on June 7, 2023, SN # 0042) to the information request (IR; sent on May 31, 2023), the Applicant clarified that monitoring of the accuracy and precision of the calibration curve during validation and sample analysis is not an assay acceptance criterion. Instead, calibration curve acceptance criteria as defined in the associated laboratory standard procedures were: STD7 must be within \pm 10\% of the nominal concentration; curve correlation coefficient R^2 \ge 0.985; replicate wells CVOD \text{Corr} \le 15.0\%. Further, the Applicant stated that human C5 standard provided in the kit is pre-prepared and not modified during the analytical procedure. The target and measured total C5 for 5 levels of standard calibrators provided by the Applicant in the IR response suggested relative bias &lt;26% (calculated by the reviewer). Considering the context of use, the Applicant's approach of the procedural verification of the calibration curve characteristics in combination with monitoring and reporting of the QC performance data are considered adequate. The total C5 samples were analyzed within the established stability window. Overall, the total C5 ELISA method validation and sample analyses reports **APPEARS THIS WAY ON ORIGINAL** are acceptable.
L1098: ## 14.3.2.2. sRBC Lysis Bioanalytical Method
L1099: For Phase 1 and 3 clinical studies, a semiquantitative method was used to test complement activation or inhibition by quantitation of hemolysis of sRBC. Complement activation or inhibition was determined by the degree of hemolysis of activated ovine erythrocytes upon exposure to plasma diluted in gelatin veronal buffer containing \text{Ca}^{2+} ad \text{Mg}^{2+} (GVB++), relative to the control plasma. The diluted plasma samples are incubated with activated ovine erythrocytes (also diluted in GVB++) at 1% final plasma content in a 37^\circ\text{C} incubator for 60 minutes. Erythrocytes are pelleted, and the optical density of supernatant (at 412 nm) is read. Percent hemolysis is obtained by subtracting the negative control background and normalizing versus the positive plasma control sample. A low percent hemolysis is indicative of complement
L1100: inhibition. The method was validated over the hemolysis range of 2% to 100% (Method 616688). For the context of use, the sRBC lysis method was validated for QC performance (precision), hemolysis effect, bench-top/process stability, freeze-thaw stability, and long-term storage stability. There is no calibration curve for this semiquantitative method. The complement activation by percent lysis in K2 EDTA plasma was stable for 785 days at -70°C. The method performance during the study sample analysis was also evaluated by QC performance (precision).
L1101: **Reviewer's Assessment:** Considering the context of use, the tested validation parameters for %sRBC lysis is appropriate to establish its use for sample analysis. The QC precision across three levels during validation was &lt;20%. The precision observed during method performance across 3 levels of QC ranged from 10.05% to 32.07%. In response (received on June 7, 2023, SN # 0042) to the information request (IR; sent on May 31, 2023), the Applicant clarified that the reference to 'measured concentration (ng/dL)' in Tables 8.7.1 and 8.7.2 in report 616688rep is confirmed to be in error and should correctly read as 'Measured %sRBC Lysis' and the stability data presented in the report are correct. The samples for %sRBC lysis were analyzed within the established stability window. Overall, the %sRBC lysis method validation and sample analyses reports are acceptable.
L1102: ## 14.4. Immunogenicity Assessment—Impact of PK/PD, Efficacy, and Safety
L1103: Zilucoplan is a 15 amino acid peptide, and it includes an ethylene glycol moiety. Although zilucoplan is smaller in size compared to protein, it could induce immunogenicity due to peptide and polyethylene glycol (PEG) moiety. Therefore, immunogenicity potential for zilucoplan was monitored by assessing both antidrug antibodies (ADA) and anti-PEG antibody (APA) in Phase 3 (MG0010, and MG0011 [open-label extension study]) studies and its impact on PK, PD, efficacy, and safety was studied. The immunogenicity (ADA response) was also evaluated in the Phase 2 study (MG0009); however, due to the drug interference in immunogenicity assay, the drug tolerance requirements were not met. The immunogenicity assay was further optimized for the Phase 3 study. Therefore, immunogenicity assessment is solely based on data from two Phase 3 studies (MG0010, and MG0011). It should also be noted that the development of assay to detect neutralizing antibody failed due to poor sensitivity and poor target and drug tolerance and hence the impact of neutralizing antibody was not evaluated. The review team opines that the neutralizing antibody assay is not needed since as presented and discussed below (1) the incidence of subjects who develop ADA and APA to zilucoplan is low which is consistent with its predicted immunogenicity potential; and (2) no changes in PK and PD were observed over time.
L1104: In study MG0010, baseline samples were collected followed by predose sampling at day 29 (week 4) and day 84 (week 12) for immunogenicity assessment (ADA and APA). In addition to efficacy and safety assessment, PK and PD samples were also collected at the above timepoints to monitor the impact of immunogenicity on zilucoplan PK, PD, efficacy, and safety. The sampling plans are appropriate for the immunogenicity assessment.
L1105: ADAs and APAs were detected using a tiered approach involving screening, confirmatory, and titer assays. ADA and APA were measured using a validated direct immune binding assays
L1106: which were assessed by the Office of Biotechnology Products (OBP; Refer to OBP's immunogenicity consult review in DARRTS dated May 4, 2023). The classification as presented in Table 86 was used to assess the ADA status. This same classification was also implemented to assess the APA status. The following sections briefly presents the Applicant's results and conclusions followed by overall reviewer's assessment.
L1107: ## 14.4.1. Incidence Rate of ADAs and APAs
L1108: In up to 12 weeks (84 days) of treatment in MG0010, 2.3% (2/86) and 9.3% (8/86) of subjects treated with zilucoplan were positive for treatment-emergent ADA and positive APA respectively (Table 87 and Table 88). The ADA titers of the two study subjects who were treatment-emergent ADA positive while treated with zilucoplan were 133 and 212. Among these two subjects, the first ADA positive result occurred at week 4 and week 12. The APA titers of eight subjects who were treatment-emergent anti-PEG positive while treated with zilucoplan were low and close to the minimum required dilution. Among these subjects, for APA, there were two first occurrences in week 1, three first occurrences in week 4, and three first occurrences in week 12.
L1109: From study MG0011, the incidence rate of treatment-emergent ADA and APA positive status in zilucoplan/zilucoplan treatment group were 2.2% (2/81) and 9.9% (8/81) respectively and was similar that of MG0090 (Table 87 and Table 88). The incidence rate of treatment-emergent ADA and APA positive status in subjects who switched from placebo to zilucoplan was 0% and 1.2% (1/84) respectively. The two subjects in MG0011 with treatment-emergent ADA positive status are the subjects from the parent study (MG0010) with occurrence at week 4 and week 12 mentioned above. Two subjects became treatment-emergent anti-PEG antibody positive during MG0011 (1 in each group – placebo/zilucoplan and zilucoplan/zilucoplan) and the APA titers were low and close to minimum required dilution in these two subjects. Among the 8 subjects with treatment-emergent APA positive status in the zilucoplan/zilucoplan treatment group, there were three first occurrences in week 12, 2 first occurrences each in week 1, and two first occurrences each in week 4, and one first occurrence in week 60. The subject with treatment-emergent anti-PEG antibody positive status in the placebo/zilucoplan treatment group had the first positive occurrence at week 72.
L1110: **Table 86. ADA Classification**
L1111: Classification	Classification label	Definition
1	Pre-ADA negative–treatment induced ADA negative	Study participants who were ADA negative at Baseline and ADA negative at all sampling points post-Baseline.
2	Pre-ADA negative–treatment induced ADA positive	Study participants who were ADA negative at Baseline and ADA positive at any sampling point post-Baseline. It also included study participants who had a missing pretreatment sample (either missing or insufficient volume) at Baseline with 1 or more ADA positive post-Baseline samples.
3	Pre-ADA positive–treatment reduced ADA	Study participants who were ADA positive at Baseline and ADA negative at all sampling points post-Baseline.
4	Pre-ADA positive–treatment unaffected ADA	Study participants who were ADA positive at Baseline and ADA positive at any sampling point post-Baseline (including Observation Period) with titer values of the same magnitude as Baseline (less than a predefined fold difference from the Baseline value, which was defined within the validation of the assay, ie, MSR of the assay).
5	Pre-ADA positive–treatment boosted ADA positive	Study participants who were ADA positive at Baseline and ADA positive at any sampling point post-Baseline (including Observation Period) with increased titer values compared to Baseline (greater than a predefined fold difference increase from Baseline value, which was defined within the validation of the assay ie, MSR of the assay).
6	Inconclusive	Study participants who had an ADA positive Baseline sample and some post-Baseline samples were missing, while other post-Baseline samples were ADA negative.
7	Treatment-emergent ADA positive	Combination of 2 and 5.
8	Pre-ADA positive	Combination of 3, 4, and 5.
L1112: ADA=antidrug antibody; MSR=minimum significant ratio

L1121: ## 14.4.2. Impact of ADAs and APAs on Zilucoplan PK
L1122: The Applicant concluded from Figure 6 and Figure 7 that ADA and APA status did not influence zilucoplan concentration. Specifically, the subjects who had treatment-emergent ADA/APA positive status had similar zilucoplan plasma concentrations compared with subjects with ADA/APA negative status. From the PK results of Study MG0011 the same above conclusion was made for the influence of ADA/APA status on zilucoplan PK (Figure 5-8 and Figure 5-11 of Integrated Summary of Immunogenicity, Module 5.3.5.3).

L1136: ## 14.4.4. Impact of ADAs and APAs on Zilucoplan Efficacy
L1137: Based on Figure 10, the Applicant concluded that the change from baseline in Myasthenia Gravis Activities of Daily Living (MG-ADL) score was numerically greater in treatment-emergent ADA positive subjects compared with the subjects who were ADA negative. Among the 7 and 6 subjects who were treatment-emergent APA positive in zilucoplan (Figure 11) and placebo treatment groups respectively, four subjects in each had at least a 3-point reduction in MG-ADL score from baseline by Week 12. The Applicant concluded that given the large variability in MG-ADL scores and the small sample size of subjects who were APA positive, no conclusion
L1138: can be drawn about the effect of APA status on MG-ADL score. From study MG0011, the Applicant concluded that change from baseline in MG-ADL score was similar in ADA/APA positive and negative subjects (Figures 5-7 and 5-10 of Integrated Summary of Immunogenicity, Module 5.3.5.3).

L1145: ## 14.4.5. Impact of ADAs and APAs on Zilucoplan Safety
L1146: The Applicant concluded that ADA and APA status do not appear to affect safety of zilucoplan. In Study MG0010, one treatment-emergent adverse event (TEAE - arrhythmia) was reported in one of two subjects starting on or after the first treatment-emergent ADA positive result in zilucoplan group. Of the two subjects who were treatment-emergent ADA positive in the placebo treatment group, no TEAEs were reported starting on or after the first treatment-emergent ADA
L1147: positive result was reported. No TEAEs of autoimmune disorders, hypersensitivity reactions, or injection site reactions were reported in subjects on or after treatment-emergent ADA positive status in either treatment group.
L1148: In 3 of 8 subjects with treatment-emergent APA positive status in zilucoplan group, 4 of 32 TEAEs were hypersensitivity reactions or injection site reactions with no TEAE in autoimmune disorders. In three of six subjects with treatment-emergent APA positive status in placebo group, nine TEAEs starting on or after the first treatment-emergent APA positive result were reported with none of them related to hypersensitivity reactions, injection site reactions, or autoimmune disorders. A similar safety observation as a function of ADA/APA status were made in Study MG0011. The proportion of subjects with TEAEs as a function of ADA/APA status does not appear to be different in zilucoplan and placebo group.
L1149: **Reviewer's Assessment:** Overall, based on the above summary, the occurrence of antidrug and anti-PEG antibodies is low. The available data are too limited to make definitive conclusions regarding immunogenicity and its impact on pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, safety, or efficacy of zilucoplan.

L1152: ## Population PK Analysis
L1153: The adult population pharmacokinetic (popPK) was developed using a dataset of 2,174 evaluable plasma concentrations from 200 subjects who received zilucoplan. A nonlinear mixed effects modeling approach with the first-order conditional estimation with interaction (FOCEI) method in nonlinear mixed-effects modeling (NONMEM), version 7.5 (ICON, Maryland) was used for the popPK analysis. An overview of the studies with available data are provided in Table 89.

L1164: ## PopPK Model
L1165: A 2-compartment model with first order absorption, followed by a target-mediated exposure enhancement model, without incorporating total complement component 5 (C5) concentration. The model was implemented using the quasi-steady-state approximation to the target-mediated drug disposition equations that solely relied on total zilucoplan concentration. Allometric coefficients were estimated to account for the effect of body weight on the linear zilucoplan PK parameters of clearance (CL), intercompartmental CL (Q), volume of distribution of the central compartment (Vc), and volume of distribution of the peripheral compartment (Vp).
L1166: The target-mediated exposure enhancement model was evaluated, and a covariate analysis was performed to determine the potential impact of covariate on CL and maximum target density (Rmax).
L1167: ## **Covariate Analysis**
L1168: A covariate analysis was performed to assess the potential effect of different demographic covariates on CL and Rmax. The following covariates were tested:
L1169: - Body weight at baseline (included as a priori for PK)
- Age at baseline
- Sex
- Race
- Population (healthy subjects versus subjects with gMG)
L1170: Based on the decrease in objective function value, effect of race on Rmax (with race categories Black/African Americans, Japanese, and all others) was the only significant covariate at the p&lt;0.05 level. Since the CIs of the effect of race on Rmax for both Black/African Americans versus all others, and for Japanese versus all others included 1 (indicating no statistical significance at p=0.05, as shown in Figure 12 and Figure 13), the relevant race categories were not considered significant and thus not included in the model.
L1171: Furthermore, the analysis demonstrated no significant association between race and overall exposure at the dose of 0.3 mg/kg, nor any effect on average steady state concentration at this dose. Consequently, the base model without covariates was considered as the final model.

Sex=females relative to males; Race=Black/African Americans and Japanese relative to all others;
Population=participants with gMG relative to healthy volunteers. Continuous covariate age is scaled to estimate the fold change across 95% of the covariate range

L1184: Parameter estimates from the final popPK model are presented in Table 91.
L1185: For a typical subject with a body weight of 70 kg, the estimated pharmacokinetic parameters were as follows: CL was 0.0845 L/hr, Vc was 3.51 L, Vp was 3.45 L, Q was 0.566 L/hr, absorption rate constant (ka) was 1.22/hr, internalization rate constant (kint) was 0.00320/hr, quasi-stationary constant (Kss) was 63.3 ng/mL, and maximum target density (Rmax) was 1780 ng/mL. The allometric exponent for body weight (WT) on CL and Q were estimated to be 0.715 and 0.452, respectively. The slope of the logarithm of ka on WT was estimated to be -1.01. Interindividual variability was observed in CL (16.8%), Vc (15.7%), Q (39.4%), ka (41.5%), and Rmax (21.5%).
L1186: The estimates of proportional and additive residual variance were deemed acceptable, with values of 9.26% and 63.3%, respectively. This indicates that the popPK model can adequately characterize the interindividual variability of these parameters (Table 91).

L1191: ## **PK Simulations**
L1192: The impact of the three administered dose categories in the MG0010 study on exposure measures was examined based on body weight. The final popPK model estimates were utilized to generate pharmacokinetic empirical bayes estimates for all records in the National Health and Nutrition Examination Survey (NHANES) database, utilizing the dosing regimen of the MG0010 study:
L1193: - 16.6 mg per day for weight &lt;56 kg
- 23.0 mg per day for weight ≥56 kg and &lt;77 kg
- 32.4 mg per day for weight ≥77 kg
L1194: The resulting exposure measures at steady state were used to derive the median and 90% of the simulated exposure measures, and these were compared with the simulated exposure measures
L1195: for all subjects in the popPK analysis. These results were graphically compared for the impact of body weight (Figure 16).
L1196: Overall, the results show that the simulated exposures are consistent across weight ranges of 25 kg to over 150 kg for all three dosing categories. These findings suggest that the intended dosing regimen adequately accounts for the influence of body weight on exposure to zilucoplan.
L1197: **Figure 16. Distribution of Steady State Zilucoplan AUC by Weight Using the Final PopPK Model**
L1198: popPK=population pharmacokinetic; ZLP=zilucoplan
Red line and blue area=median and 90% of simulated values for patients sampled from the Nhanes database. Orange circles=individual predicted values; vertical black lines=weights (56kg and 77kg) where dose changes from 16.6mg to 23mg to 32.4mg
L1199: Source: The Applicant's popPK report, Page 165, Figure 88

L1236: ## 14.6. Pharmacogenetics
L1237: Not Applicable
L1238: ## 15. Study/Trial Design
L1239: ## 15.1. Study MG0009
L1240: This study was a dose-finding study and is not included in the proposed labeling. Its design and population were very similar to study MG0010. The primary endpoint of change from baseline on the QMG was not statistically significant. QMG and MG-ADL (key secondary endpoint) scores had mean changes over 12 weeks that were higher in the zilucoplan groups than placebo (-6.0 versus -3.2 for QMG; and -3.4 versus -1.1 for MG-ADL). Data from this study informed the safety review and the clinical pharmacology review.
L1241: ## 15.1.1. Design, Study MG0009
L1242: Study MG0009 was a two-part, multicenter, dose-ranging study in 44 subjects to evaluate the safety, tolerability, and preliminary efficacy of once daily subcutaneous zilucoplan. Subjects were randomly assigned (1:1:1) to 12 weeks of placebo (n=15), zilucoplan 0.1 mg/kg (n=15), or zilucoplan 0.3 mg/kg (n=14) after stratification by screening QMG score. Subjects were expected to remain on stable doses of all other medications for MG unless otherwise medically indicated.
L1243: The open-label portion of study MG0009 consisted of 42 subjects from the placebo-controlled period, who did not have study drug permanently discontinued. Subjects on the 0.3 mg/kg dose in the placebo-controlled portion continued on 0.3 mg/kg in the open-label extension; subjects on 0.1 mg/kg continued on 0.1 mg/kg; and subjects on placebo were randomized (1:1) to 0.1 mg/kg or 0.3 mg/kg. However, during the OL phase of this study, all subjects were changed to the zilucoplan 0.3 mg/kg dose pursuant to a protocol amendment.
L1244: ## 15.1.2. Eligibility Criteria, Study MG0009
L1245: The eligibility criteria for Study MG0009 were the same as for Study MG0010, except that MG0009 did not set a minimum MG-ADL score for enrollment. However, subjects in MG0009 and MG0010 had similar mean screening MG-ADL and QMG scores.
L1246: The main eligibility criteria for the open-label extension of study MG0009 were completion of the placebo-controlled period without permanent discontinuation of zilucoplan.
L1247: ## 15.1.3. Statistical Analysis Plan, Study MG0009
L1248: The primary efficacy endpoint was the change from baseline (CFB) to week 12 in the QMG score. The key secondary efficacy endpoints were:
L1249: - Change from baseline to week 12 in the MG-ADL total score
- Change from baseline to week 12 in the MGC total score
- Change from baseline to week 12 in the MG-QOL15r Survey
- The number of subjects receiving rescue therapy over 12 weeks
- The number of subjects with MG-ADL total of 0 or 1 at week 12
- The number of subjects achieving 3-point or greater reduction in QMG at week 12
L1250: ## 15.1.4. Demographics, Study MG0009
L1251: The majority of subjects enrolled in MG0009 completed the study, and most completers entered the extension study.
L1252: Subjects were balanced demographically, including by sex. Subjects resembled the wider MG population (mean age 50 years, 16% &gt;65 years, BMI 33 kg/m2, 80% white) and were balanced between groups.
L1253: The two groups were generally balanced in terms of comorbidities (43% had previous/ongoing psychiatric SOC; 28% previous/ongoing infectious SOC.). Commonly reported comorbid medical conditions by PT were hypertension (52%), depression (27%), and diabetes (18%). Sleep apnea was one PT that was imbalanced (13.3% in PLO and low dose zilucoplan, 57% in high dose zilucoplan), though it is unlikely to have introduced a type 1 error.
L1254: Baseline myasthenia characteristics were balanced between groups and similar to MG0010 (mean age at disease onset of 41 years, mean disease duration 8.4 years, median MG-ADL 7, median QMG 18, 98% were on concomitant MG medications [91% on cholinesterase inhibitors, 91% on steroids, 61% on immunoglobulin]).
L1255: ## 16. Efficacy
L1256: Not Applicable.
L1257: ## 17. Clinical Safety
L1258: ## 17.1. QT Prolongation
L1259: No significant QTcF prolongation effect of zilucoplan has been identified.
L1260: The effect of zilucoplan was evaluated in study # UP0093, which was a Phase 1, double-blind, randomized, placebo- and active-controlled, multiple-dose, single-center, parallel group with nested crossover study of the effects of supratherapeutic dose of zilucoplan on cardiac repolarization in healthy adult study participants. The highest dose evaluated was 0.6 mg/kg, which covered the anticipated high clinical exposure in study UP0093. Data were analyzed using exposure-response analysis as the primary analysis, which did not suggest that zilucoplan is associated with significant QTc prolonging effects. Findings of this analysis are further supported by the by-time point analysis that excluded the 10-msec threshold and by the lack of outliers in the categorical analysis.
L1261: ## 17.2. Laboratory Pancreatic Enzyme Analyses
L1262: Figure 23 shows the Applicant's plot of mean serum lipase levels in study MG0010 by visit (day) and treatment group. The nominal 95% confidence intervals (CIs) in Figure 23 suggest a significant difference between the zilucoplan 0.3 mg/kg and placebo groups. Serum amylase also showed a peak at day 57 but smaller, and not shown in this figure.
L1263: **Figure 23. Study MG0010: Mean (95% CI) Change in Serum Lipase Level by Visit and Treatment Group.**
L1264: Source: Applicant's Figure.
L1265: As shown in **Figure 23**, the zilucoplan group had a 44 U/L increase in mean lipase level between baseline and day 57 (mean lipase went from 56 U/L to 104 U/L, not shown), as compared to 2 U/L in the placebo group. At day 84, mean lipase remained above baseline by 27 U/L, while the placebo group stayed 4 U/L above baseline. Although the day 57 mean included a single value of 1,987 U/L, removal of that data point had little impact on the appearance of the curve. Other subjects had serum lipase levels that also exceeded 3x ULN that were not called an AE. Most of these data were not at a level of detail to determine whether abdominal pain was present, or if imaging was done. However, if abdominal pain had been present, the American College of Gastroenterology criteria5 (Tenner et al. 2013) for acute pancreatitis could have been met.
L1266: Another trend in **Figure 23** is the large increase in variability of lipase levels in the zilucoplan group beginning at day 57 and persisting through day 84. This means that a sizeable number of subjects still had serum lipase levels exceeding their baseline values, or newly developed them at days 57 and/or 87, despite the decreasing mean.
L1267: In study MG0010, the ULN for lipase varied from 100 to 130 U/L, depending on the laboratory.
L1268: **Table 94** is a shift table for subjects with normal (N) lipase at baseline. No subjects in the placebo group had lipase &gt;3x ULN.
L1269: 5 American College of Gastroenterology (ACG) criteria for acute pancreatitis: “The diagnosis of AP is most often established by the presence of two of the three following criteria: (i) abdominal pain consistent with the disease, (ii) serum amylase and/or lipase greater than three times the upper limit of normal, and/or (iii) characteristic findings from abdominal imaging.”

L1302: In Figure 25, the nominal 95% CIs are not shown, as they overlap considerably at all visits due to the small group sizes. Also similar is the net increase from baseline to day 84, which is 20 U/L for study MG0009 compared to 27 U/L for study MG0010.
L1303: For placebo subjects in MG0009 who rolled into the MG0009 OLE, the peak mean lipase was at day 84 but had started to increase at day 57. This was likely related to not all patients in MG0009 OLE being on the 0.3 mg/kg dose because of a protocol amendment during the study, as follows. At the end of the double-blind period of MG0009, placebo subjects were randomized 1:1 to zilucoplan 0.1 mg/kg or 0.3 mg/kg, and then all subjects in all extensions (including MG0011) were changed to 0.3 mg/kg at some point pursuant to a protocol amendment. Therefore, the actual overall zilucoplan exposure in the [original] placebo arm of Study MG0009 exceeded 0.1 mg/kg but was less than 0.3 mg/kg during the extension, and this may explain the delayed peak.

L1316: In the double-blind period of study MG0009 and not shown in the table is that some subjects had high (H) lipase level at baseline – 3 placebo, 5 zilucoplan 0.1 mg/kg, and 3 zilucoplan 0.3 mg/kg. Of the 3 PBO subjects, 2 remained high (H) at day 84; of the 6 zilucoplan 0.1 mg/kg subjects, 5 remained high (H) at day 84; and of the 3 zilucoplan 0.3 mg/kg subjects, all three subjects remained high (H) at day 84.
L1317: Amylase Level &gt;3x ULN in the Laboratory Data. The above discussion addresses lipase only, as it is more sensitive and specific than amylase for pancreatitis. However, considering that amylase &gt;3xULN is one of the American College of Gastroenterology criteria for acute pancreatitis, the cases of high-amylase AEs and amylase elevations in the laboratory data were examined as well.
L1318: There were two lab values with serum amylase &gt;3xULN, both in study MG0011—one subject who had been on placebo in MG0010 had a high amylase level (785 U/L) at visit E8 (day 8 of MG0011), and the other who had been on placebo in MG0009 and had a high amylase level (321 U/L) at visit E108.

L1327: ## 22. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L1328: There were data integrity issues in the narratives and pooled (ISS) and individual analysis dataset of adverse events and ADLB datasets. At least seven IRs were issued between January and July 2023 for these datasets. The problems involved all three studies and pooled datasets. They included missing data and records (including one death from pancreatic cancer—subject [REDACTED](b) (6), blank treatment-emergent flags, irreconcilable AE record counts, erroneously low values for liver and renal lab data and eosinophil counts in study MG0010, deviations that appeared in the pdf listing but not the deviations dataset. Most of the important issues with the narrative data was resolved. AE record counts changed with each amendment, and there was difficulty reconciling between or within study AE counts.
L1329: The Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI) identified several protocol deviations at three sites, although OSI assessed that study MG0010 appeared to have been conducted adequately and the data generated from these sites appeared acceptable for this indication. There was no evidence of under-reporting of adverse events. Several protocol deviations occurring at Site #119 involved 3 (21%) of 14 subjects taking anticholinergic inhibitors &lt;10 hours before administration of the MG-ADL and QMG. According to onsite documents, these deviations were not identified until the date of database lock one to two years after the deviations had occurred. The statistical reviewer did a sensitivity analysis of the effect of these deviations on the primary endpoint and found that they did not change the statistical decision to reject the null hypothesis in favor of zilucoplan.
L1330: OSI issued an information request to the Applicant to submit data clarification forms (DCF) and audit trails for changes to the MG-ADL and QMG scores. OSI found that changes to MG-ADL and QMG scores occurred in 20 (11.5%) of 174 randomized subjects for 27 study visits. The majority of data changes were for QMG scores and were related to spirometry data (e.g., forced vital capacity). Overall, there were few data changes, and only one pertained to the primary endpoint efficacy data (MG-ADL scores at the timepoints of interest).

L1339: ## PMR 4487-1
L1340: Conduct a worldwide descriptive study that collects prospective and retrospective data in women exposed to zilucoplan during pregnancy and/or lactation to assess risk of pregnancy and maternal complications, adverse effects on the developing fetus and neonate, and adverse effects on the infant. Infant outcomes will be assessed through at least the first year of life. The minimum number of patients will be specified in the protocol.

L1408: Figure 5 is a schematic diagram illustrating the study design for Study # UP0115, a two-way incomplete crossover study comparing three injection sites (abdomen, arm, thigh) in two groups (A and B).
The study flow is as follows:
Group A: n=8 subjects. Screening leads to Randomization. The subjects are assigned to one of two sequences:
Sequence 1: Zilucoplan (abdomen A) followed by Zilucoplan (arm).
Sequence 2: Zilucoplan (arm) followed by Zilucoplan (abdomen A).
Group B: n=8 subjects. Screening leads to Randomization. The subjects are assigned to one of two sequences:
Sequence 1: Zilucoplan (abdomen B) followed by Zilucoplan (thigh).
Sequence 2: Zilucoplan (thigh) followed by Zilucoplan (abdomen B).
All subjects proceed through Treatment Period 1 (35 days), a Rest Period (7 days), and Treatment Period 2 (35 days), concluding at EOS (End of Study).

For all covariates, the point estimate (dot) is greater than 1.0, and the 95% confidence interval (CI) bars extend above 1.0, indicating a positive effect.
L1417: Figure 14 displays four Goodness-of-Fit (GoF) plots for the Final PopPK Model for Zilucoplan. The plots are arranged in a 2x2 grid, showing observed versus predicted concentrations and residuals.
Top Left Plot: Observed Zilucoplan concentration (ng/mL) versus Predicted Zilucoplan concentration (ng/mL). The data points are scattered around the 45-degree line (black line), and a blue loess smoother is fitted, indicating a good fit.
Top Right Plot: Observed Zilucoplan concentration (ng/mL) versus Individual Predicted Zilucoplan concentration (IPRED, ng/mL). The data points are scattered around the 45-degree line (black line), and a blue loess smoother is fitted, indicating a good fit.
Bottom Left Plot: CWRESI (Conditional Weighted Residuals) versus Predicted Zilucoplan concentration (PRED, ng/mL). The CWRESI values are centered around zero (black line), and a blue loess smoother is fitted, showing no systematic trend.
Bottom Right Plot: CWRESI (Conditional Weighted Residuals) versus Time after dose (h). The CWRESI values are centered around zero (black line), and a blue loess smoother is fitted, showing no systematic trend over time.
The darkness of the hexagons corresponds to the data density at that location.
L1418: Figure 15 displays six plots showing the predicted Variability of Population Pharmacokinetics (pVPCs) for Zilucoplan concentration (ng/mL) versus Time after dose (h) on a logarithmic scale, stratified by dose (0.05 mg/kg, 0.1 mg/kg, 0.2 mg/kg, 0.3 mg/kg, 0.4 mg/kg, and 0.6 mg/kg). The plots compare observed data (red circles) with simulated quantiles (black lines and shaded areas).
The Y-axis (Zilucoplan concentration in ng/mL) is logarithmic, ranging from 100 to 10000. The X-axis (Time after dose in h) ranges from 0 to 40 hours for the top four plots and up to 60 hours for the bottom two plots.
Legend (applies to all plots):
Red lines = observed zilucoplan quantiles (2.5th, 50th, 97.5th) across simulated trials.
Black lines = median of zilucoplan quantiles (2.5th, 50th, 97.5th) across simulated trials.
Blue and red shaded areas = 95% of the zilucoplan quantiles (2.5th, 50th, 97.5th) across simulated trials.
Red circles = observations.
The plots show that Zilucoplan concentration peaks rapidly after dose administration (around 1-2 hours) and then declines. The median concentration and the 95% prediction interval generally decrease over time, with the 95% interval widening slightly over time, especially for higher doses (0.4 mg/kg and 0.6 mg/kg).

L1421: Figure 18 displays graphical analyses of random effects (ETA) for Clearance (CL), Volume of Distribution (VC), Clearance/Volume (Q), and Clearance/Volume/Protein (VP) against Age, Sex, and Race, based on a PopPK Model using only Weight Allometric Scaling.
The figure is organized into four rows, corresponding to ETA-CL, ETA-VC, ETA-Q, and ETA-VP, and three columns, corresponding to Age (20 to 60 years), Sex (Female, Male), and Race (White, Black, Japanese, Asian, Other, NA).
For Age, scatter plots show ETA values versus AGE (yr). The scatter plots for ETA-CL, ETA-VC, ETA-Q, and ETA-VP all show a wide distribution of points centered around 0.0, with a horizontal blue line indicating the mean. The sample size is 116 for ETA-CL and ETA-VC, and 83 for ETA-Q and ETA-VP.
For Sex, box plots show ETA values for Female (n=116) and Male (n=83). The distributions for ETA-CL, ETA-VC, ETA-Q, and ETA-VP are centered around 0.0, with minimal spread.
For Race, box plots show ETA values for White (n=134), Black (n=32), Japanese (n=14), Asian (n=11), Other (n=1), and NA (n=7). The distributions for ETA-CL, ETA-VC, ETA-Q, and ETA-VP are centered around 0.0, with minimal spread.
Overall, the figure suggests that age, sex, and race do not have a clinically significant impact on the random effects of these PK parameters when weight allometric scaling is applied.

L1424: Figure 21 displays three line graphs showing the Mean (95%CI) Change of MG-ADL Score from Baseline over Time after dose (weeks) for three weight groups: \ge 43\text{kg} < 56\text{kg}, = 56\text{kg} < 77\text{kg}, and = 77\text{kg} < 150\text{kg}. The Y-axis represents the Change of MG-ADL Score from Baseline, ranging from -6 to 3. The X-axis represents Time after dose (wks), ranging from 0 to 12. The legend indicates two treatment groups: 0.3 mg/kg zilucoplan (red line) and Placebo (teal line).
Weight Group 1: \ge 43\text{kg} < 56\text{kg}
Placebo (Teal line): Starts near 0, peaks around 2 weeks (approx. +1.5), and ends around 12 weeks (approx. +0.5).
Zilucoplan (Red line): Starts near 0, decreases steadily, reaching approximately -6 at 12 weeks.
Weight Group 2: = 56\text{kg} < 77\text{kg}
Placebo (Teal line): Starts near 0, remains relatively stable, ending around -0.5 at 12 weeks.
Zilucoplan (Red line): Starts near 0, decreases steadily, reaching approximately -5 at 12 weeks.
Weight Group 3: = 77\text{kg} < 150\text{kg}
Placebo (Teal line): Starts near 0, peaks around 2 weeks (approx. +1.5), and ends around 12 weeks (approx. -0.5).
Zilucoplan (Red line): Starts near 0, decreases steadily, reaching approximately -5 at 12 weeks.
In all weight groups, the zilucoplan group shows a significantly greater decrease in MG-ADL score compared to the placebo group over the 12-week period.

L1427: Line graph showing Long-Term Mean Serum Lipase (U/L) by Treatment Group in MG0010. The X-axis represents Weeks since randomization in MG0010 (0 to 108). The Y-axis represents Mean Lipase (U/L) (0 to 120). Two lines are plotted: PBO (Placebo, blue line) and ZLP 0.3 mg/kg (Zilucoplan, orange line). Both groups show a peak lipase level around Week 8 (Day 57). The ZLP group peaks around 100 U/L, while the PBO group peaks around 80 U/L. After the peak, both groups show a general decline in mean lipase levels, although the ZLP group maintains a higher mean lipase level than the PBO group for the remainder of the study period shown.
L1428: Line graph titled 'Study MG0009 DB-OLE: Mean Lipase (U/L) by Day'. The Y-axis is Mean Lipase (U/L) from 0 to 120. The X-axis is Total Days in MG0009 from 0 to 180. The graph shows data for four groups: ZLP 0.1 mg/kg, ZLP 0.3 mg/kg, PBO, and PBO/ZLP 0.1-0.3 mg/kg. The ZLP 0.3 mg/kg group shows a peak mean lipase around Day 57 (approximately 95 U/L). The PBO group shows a peak mean lipase around Day 84 (approximately 90 U/L). The ZLP 0.1 mg/kg and PBO/ZLP 0.1-0.3 mg/kg groups show lower mean lipase levels, generally below 70 U/L.
